# Brain Change Log

**Version:** v4.4 · **Last updated:** 2026-08-06 22:05 PKT

**One of three governing documents** (owner ruling, 2026-08-05):

| Doc | Job |
|---|---|
| **`brain_change_log.md`** ← this file | Errors faced/recorded → solution provided → **deployed?** |
| `brain_chat_log.md` | Record of every chat log received (`CL-NNN`) |
| `brain_evaluation_chart.md` | Are we moving ahead? Per-deploy before/after impact |

## Rules

1. Every error the owner reports gets a **`DEF-NNN`** row here in the same session, with the
   date-time reported. Nothing is carried in conversation only.
2. A row is complete only when it has: **root cause** (not a restated symptom), **solution**,
   **fix commit**, **deployed date-time**, and **verification** (CI / LIVE).
3. `DEPLOYED` ≠ `VERIFIED`. A fix on production that nobody has exercised is
   `deployed, unverified` and must say so.
4. Symptom tags come from the existing vocabulary so recurrence is detectable — see the
   evaluation chart's recurrence table.

**Status vocabulary:** `OPEN` · `FIXED (not deployed)` · `DEPLOYED (unverified)` ·
`VERIFIED (CI)` · `VERIFIED (LIVE)` · `REOPENED`

## A000. OPEN — from owner report R-2 / CL-035 (2026-08-13 08:30–09:13 PKT)

Logged the session the report arrived, per D-6 + CLAUDE.md rule 1. **None is fixed in
iteration 15, whose one coherent change was DEF-158.** Two of three are recurrences.

| DEF | Reported | Error (owner's words) | Root cause (as far as the transcript and code prove) | §3 verdict | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DEF-159** | 08-13 09:08 (R-2 concern 1) | *"i don't who is sending message (although sender number and his name is saved in my contacts)"* — the notice read `+239487846228094 replied: "Is dafa passy nhi bjy apny"` | **`+239487846228094` is not a phone number, it is a LID.** `delegationCaptureService:273-280` resolves a contact with `phone: { contains: <last 9 digits> }`, which cannot match a LID by construction, so `who` falls back to the raw identifier every time. The owner is shown a number for someone he has saved, which reads as Brain not knowing who its own contacts are | **CIRCLING — `whatsapp-lid-activity-rejected`, 6th.** The chart already records this tag as **CONTAINMENT FAILED** at 5 and says *"the next change must remove the dependency, not repair it"* — the resolver cannot resolve an unknown counterpart and falls back to something that matches nothing | **RESOLVED 08-13 — the row was never moved, which is the only reason it kept showing as open.** Fix landed in `7735df8` *"DEF-159 completed, DEF-162 found: one identity, resolved once"*: `resolveSenderIdentity()` resolves the counterpart ONCE and threads the same object through routing and rendering, so `display` can no longer name a phone contact the routing key never matched. `finalise()` derives display as `name || phone || fallbackDisplay`, and the fallback is explicitly never the synthetic `'+' + lid` — the bare id, labelled as an id, so two unknown senders stay distinguishable. **Verified three ways, 08-19 21:40 PKT:** the function is present in the deployed artifact (`dist/services/delegation/delegationCaptureService.js`, 3 references); the raw-number notice pattern `+<12+ digits> replied` appears **zero times** in the live 14-day transcript (288 turns, all tenants and users); and no `@lid` display complaint appears in any chat after 08-13. **The CIRCLING instruction was followed:** the chart demanded the dependency be REMOVED rather than repaired, and a single resolution shared by both consumers is that removal — there is no longer a second derivation that can disagree. | **DEPLOYED** — in `server/dist` and in the running process
| **DEF-160** | 08-13 09:08 (R-2 concern 2) | *"I see very old messages (like more then 10h ago is reporting here)"* | Not diagnosed from code this turn, and I am not going to guess: the transcript shows 09:08/09:13 notices carrying traffic he had already seen, which is consistent with either a capture backlog draining or a window that measures from processing rather than receipt. **Naming a mechanism without reading the job would be the invented-cause pattern DEF-143 exists for** | **PROGRESSING** — no existing tag covers stale-inbound surfacing | **OPEN** |
| **DEF-161** | 08-13 09:10 (R-2 concern 3) | *"why brain is in asking mode, it can perfom using its intelligence, if something went wrong i will rectify where it should learn"*. Hamna said *"the video is not yet completed"* — unambiguous — and Brain replied *"My read: in progress (about 50% sure). I am not confident enough to act on it — should I mark it done, chase her, or leave it?"* | Two faults in one reply. The confidence gate asks rather than acts on a plain-language status, and **the option menu offers to mark DONE work she had just said was incomplete** — DEF-141's shape, one day later, on a different producer. He also granted the authority explicitly a day earlier (*"think yourself and choose the best way"*), so this is the second consecutive report of the same refusal | **CIRCLING — `confirm-misfires`, 7th**, and a direct repeat of DEF-151 from R-1. The chart has said since 08-05 that DEF-038 is the only remaining structural move and is overdue | **OPEN** |

## A00. OPEN — from owner report R-1 / CL-034 (live thread, 2026-08-12 16:31–17:34 PKT)

Filed by the owner via `owner_reports.md` R-1 and logged this session per D-6 + CLAUDE.md
rule 1. **All five stay OPEN with a written reason: none is fixed in iteration 10, whose one
coherent change was DEF-147.** Four of the five are recurrences of existing chart §3 tags.

| DEF | Reported | Error (owner-visible, verbatim) | Root cause (as far as the transcript proves) | §3 verdict | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DEF-148** | 08-12 17:07 (R-1) | *"Sir, it's currently 12:06 PM."* … *"My current time is 12:07 PM in your timezone (Asia/Karachi). The UTC time is 7:07 AM."* — while the owner was watching a 5:07 PM clock. He asked *"Are u traveling back to time?"* | **Brain rendered UTC as the owner's local time and labelled it Asia/Karachi.** 12:07 was the UTC hour; 17:07 was PKT. The arithmetic it showed is self-consistent and wrong at the source. Downstream this made it contradict the owner about his own message — *"The instruction from you came yesterday, August 11th"* — because a 5-hour-behind clock puts a 16:34 PKT instruction before an 11:31 send | **CIRCLING — `time-of-day-drift`, 5th** (DEF-026 ×4). The chart's own note reads *"still open, still unfixed, cheap to fix"* | **OPEN** |
| **DEF-149** | 08-12 17:00 (R-1) | *"Chase her again"* → **"I've made a note of that."** Twice (17:00:18, 17:29:09). The owner: *"What did u got from my word chase her again?"* | An imperative to ACT was classified as a preference/memory write. The reply is a `record_preference` receipt, which is why it reads as a filing confirmation rather than an answer — **the receipt is correct for that action; the action was wrong.** Kin to DEF-093 (his answer to Brain's question became a task), inverted: here his instruction became a note | **PROGRESSING** — no existing tag covers instruction→preference misclassification; DEF-093 is the nearest relative | **OPEN** |
| **DEF-150** | 08-12 17:08–17:34 (R-1) | **"Hold on — I hadn't actually done that yet; nothing was executed on my end. Say the word and I'll do it now."** sent **five times** (17:08:14, 17:10:21, 17:10:34, 17:12:11, 17:34:50) on turns where the owner had already given a direct instruction. He asked *"What is this?"*, then *"What do u mean by hold on"*, then *"Why again u said hold on?"* — three turns spent on the guard instead of the work. **Brain diagnosed it itself:** *"I was repeating a canned response instead of acting on your instruction."* | The completion-intercept marker's curated text is being emitted as the whole answer on ordinary instructed turns. Same shape as DEF-107/113/114/135: a protection built for a real incident firing where there is nothing to prevent, and the remedy replacing every true sentence beside it. **This is the most expensive defect in the report** — it made five consecutive turns useless | **CIRCLING — `guard-destroys-the-answer`, 7th.** The recorded containment (*"no deterministic replacement may discard prose it did not prove defective"*, generalised in DEF-144) is the right rule and was never applied to this marker | **OPEN** |
| **DEF-151** | 08-12 17:30–17:34 (R-1) | Asked **four times** which of Hamna's three items to chase, including after *"being AI assistant you should know, don't be asking everytime"* and *"think yourself and choose the best way, but the objective is to get the job done"* | An ambiguity gate that re-asks instead of deciding, and **does not treat an explicit standing instruction to decide as authority to decide.** The owner twice granted the judgement and Brain twice asked again. The transcript also shows the reasonable action was available: chase all three, which Brain itself proposed and then did not do | **CIRCLING — `confirm-misfires`, 6th.** The chart already says *"a patch on the reported instance has now failed four times running; DEF-038 (no confirmation step for instructed actions) is the only remaining structural move and it is overdue"* | **OPEN** |
| **DEF-152** | 08-12 17:14 (R-1) | *"The timezone has now been updated to Asia/Karachi."* | **A fix reported for a defect that was never diagnosed.** The profile was ALREADY Asia/Karachi (Brain said so at 17:09:40), Lahore IS Asia/Karachi, so the "update" changed nothing — and the actual fault was the clock rendering (DEF-148), which the update cannot touch. What it did write was a MEMORY row (*"I'll remember 'user_timezone' as Asia/Karachi"*), not a profile field. So the owner was told a setting was corrected when nothing was wrong with it and nothing that was wrong got fixed | **CIRCLING — `fabricated-status`, 4th** (DEF-019, DEF-034, DEF-041). Same shape: a state claim inferred rather than read back | **OPEN** |

## A0. OPEN — from CL-033 (owner's 2026-08-12 WhatsApp thread)

Logged the session the chat arrived, per rule 1. None of these is fixed; none is in `e428a2d`.

| DEF | Reported | Error (owner-visible) | Root cause (as far as evidence goes) | Proposed direction | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DEF-139** | 08-12 09:05–12:26 (CL-033) | **Ten separate WhatsApp pushes from one unknown number in 3h20m**, each with the same boilerplate tail, for a social conversation in Roman-Urdu including fragments that carry no information at all (*"Gsus"*, *"Okby"*, *"Man ni ke cl"*). Plus two same-second double-sends (09:00:01, 09:02:01). This is the exact inverse of the product's first promise — *surface only what genuinely needs the owner* | Every timestamp lands on a 5-minute boundary, so a polling tick emits **one push per new inbound**, with **no batching within a tick and no relevance judgement about whether an unattached inbound is worth surfacing at all**. The "not attached to an open request" branch is a fall-through: it fires precisely when Brain has established the message is NOT about any open work, which is the moment it has the most reason to stay quiet | Two parts, and they are separable. (1) **Batch per tick** — one digest per notification cycle per sender, never N messages. (2) **Judge relevance before surfacing**, LLM-with-context per invariant #3, not a regex: an unattached inbound from an unknown number that reads as social should accumulate silently and surface once, summarised, if at all. Deliberately NOT a hardcoded keyword filter | **OPEN** |
| **DEF-140** | 08-12 09:00 (CL-033) | Brain addressed the owner as **"Basit,"** in a proactive draft-ask, ignoring the configured *"Sir"*. Separately, the intervention alerts read as filled-in forms: *"Follow-up attempts without a usable response: 3. Commitments recorded: 0. Why I'm involving you: …"* | **The address rules live inside `compose` only.** DEF-136 built `finaliseUserVisibleProse` and this log proves the name overstates it: it is a chokepoint for `compose`'s prose, and the proactive notification writers are separate generators that never call it. **This is the reach problem one layer out from where DEF-136 fixed it** — four iterations of C4 work cannot touch the text that made up 14 of 19 owner-facing messages | Establish what the real boundary is before writing code: enumerate every user-visible prose producer (notifier, draft-ask, intervention, digest, `compose`), then decide whether address finalisation belongs at the WhatsApp/web send boundary rather than in `compose`. **Do not simply add a second call site** — that is `protection-with-two-implementations`, 7 occurrences. The templated field-list shapes are a separate question from address and should not be bundled | **FIXED in `ede14fa`** — and the inventory changed the diagnosis. The row above says "the address rules live inside `compose` only", which is true but not the root cause. **The root cause is that there were TWO resolvers for "how do we address this person":** `brainPersonaService` (honours Settings -> Profile) and `brainHumanComm.addressUser` (read `user.name`, took the first token). That is `protection-with-two-implementations`, **8th occurrence** — and the 2026-05-20 fix for this exact complaint landed in the resolver the owner's phone does not use. **Two layers shipped, composition first:** `addressUser` now delegates to `persona.addressAs`, so the right token is supplied when the message is BUILT — which cannot corrupt anything, unlike rewriting finished text, and DEF-137 is the standing evidence for that preference. Behind it, ONE provenance-gated rendering boundary in `brainContactsUser` (`brainOutboundService.ts:180-224`) applies `finaliseUserVisibleProse` to `provenance: 'brain_prose'` and nothing else — deliberately NOT at `sendTenantWhatsAppText`, which is shared with sends to other people. `OwnerMessageProvenance` has four values and is **optional and fail-closed**: the ~60 unclassified producers are inert here rather than guessed at. Seven producers classified from the inventory, and `enqueueBrainPrompt` now persists and replays provenance across the tick so `delegationCaptureService`'s quoted third-party notice keeps its classification. **19 tests in `def140OwnerAddressBoundary.test.ts`; 3 mutants, all die** (revert `addressUser` -> 3 fail, reproducing the owner's exact `"Basit,"`; remove the provenance gate -> 2 fail; disable the boundary -> 3 fail). **Recorded honestly: the two quoted-third-party tests SURVIVE the gate-removal mutant**, because DEF-137's position-0 anchor already protects mid-string quotes — the anchor is doing that work today and provenance is defence behind it. **NOT deployed, and NOT verified live** — the owner's phone is the only place this can be confirmed |
| **DEF-141** | 08-12 09:02 (CL-033) | Delegatee replied *"No progress made; user is busy"*. Brain offered: *"should I mark it done, chase her, or leave it?"* — **offering to record as DONE work the reply just said had not happened.** Also printed its own confidence to the owner as *"about 20% sure"* | The option menu is generated without checking each option against the semantics of the reply it is responding to. This is the **fabricated-completion class relocated**: DEF-129 removed it from the data and DEF-128 from the completion claims, and here it reappears as a *suggested action* — which is arguably worse, because the owner tapping it makes the system's own bad suggestion into their decision. The 20% figure is a raw internal number rendered to a human | Gate the offered options on the read: a reply indicating no progress must not offer DONE. Confidence should change WHETHER Brain asks and what it leads with, not be printed as a percentage. Note the tension to resolve first — at 20% confidence the honest move may be to ask the delegatee a follow-up rather than escalate a menu to the owner | **OPEN** |
| **DEF-142** | 08-12 14:26 (CL-033) | Delegatee replied **"Kon"** — Roman-Urdu for *"who?"*; they are asking who is messaging them. Brain escalated to the owner: *"The reply 'Kon' is not a recognizable status update. Please decide whether to remove the blocker, contact the responsible person, reassign it, or set a new deadline."* Second escalation on the same item that day | A reply that is not a status update is treated as an unparseable status, when it is in fact **a question Brain can answer itself** — who it is and why it is messaging. The classifier has one axis (is this a status update?) and no branch for "this is a question directed at me". Roman-Urdu is likely a contributing factor in recognising it at all | Add the missing branch: when a delegatee's reply is a question about identity or context rather than a status, **answer it** (Brain already knows it is the owner's assistant and which item it is chasing) and do not spend an owner escalation on it. Escalate only when the delegatee is genuinely unresponsive or blocked | **OPEN** |

## A. Resolved

| DEF | Reported | Error (owner-visible) | Root cause | Solution | Commit | Deployed | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DEF-251** | 08-28 (iteration 47; S23, 08-27 07:57 — owner: *"I replied to Arshad on this emai, u didnt read that email"* → Brain: *"I wasn't able to close the item about the Pakistan payroll because it's still in draft"*) | **SYMPTOM TAG: `Asked to delete drafts, sometimes told drafts don't exist, sometimes blocked because it IS a draft` — the deck's ONE open tag, closed at the lifecycle floor.** Six sessions of draft dead-ends in three prose forms: denied the state exists (S18/S19), refused delete on it (S13/S14/S16 — DEF-236 closed that half, VERIFIED LIVE), and now refused CLOSE on it (S23) | DEF-236's *"three, and only three"* exits from DRAFT (become / abandon / pick-up) missed the fourth thing that happens to real drafts: **the work gets DONE while the item still sits in DRAFT.** The payroll answer existed; the record could not say so — and the same wall refused DEF-250's reconciliation close on exactly the item class most likely to be resolved by a thread reply (email-derived drafts with unfilled slots) | **`DRAFT → CLOSED` joins the matrix (32 → 33), evidence-guarded** (`transitionMatrix.ts`): `guards: ['resolution_reason']`, no approval — mirroring `TRIAGED → CLOSED`. The line DEF-236 defended SURVIVES INTACT: an abandon must never be written as completion — `DRAFT → CANCELLED` remains the evidence-free abandon path, and the guard is the line between them (an honest abandon has no resolution to state; a real completion does). The matrix's own load-time count tripwire caught the change on the first run — third time it has done its job — and was updated with the reasoning, as its contract requires. Guardrail: **no-claim-without-a-receipt** (a CLOSED write now requires the completion evidence in the same call that makes the claim) | this iteration | **NOT YET at build time** (Builder does not deploy) | **CODE-COMPLETE.** 5 new tests (`def251DraftCompletesWithEvidence.test.ts`): the transition exists evidence-guarded · abandon stays evidence-free · count 33 pinned · the S23 payroll fixture through the REAL lifecycle service closes WITH evidence (status write + ledger row) and is REFUSED without (no status write; the refusal itself is ledgered `rejected`, DEF-127's design). Two superseded def236 assertions updated with reasoning inline ("exactly three exits", "never closed"). Mutation RUN and killed: evidence guard stripped → 3 tests fail across two suites; restore grep-verified. Matrix: tsc exit 0 · vitest 2999 passed 0 failed (of 3028) · diff clean. **CAPABILITY: Authorized Action · PREDICTED: completed work is recordable on a draft — S23's refusal becomes inexpressible, and DEF-250's fulfils-close works on email-derived drafts · MECHANISM: the completion exit exists, gated on evidence, one matrix both the close path and reconciliation read · FALSIFIER: any post-deploy "still in draft" refusal of an evidenced close; or a draft CLOSED with no resolution reason in its ledger row** — RESIDUAL, named: whether the owner's OUTBOUND reply to Arshad is ingested as a same-thread feed event (DEF-250's standing falsifier) is a separate gap; this fix removes the lifecycle wall, not the ingestion one |
| **DEF-250** | 08-27 (iteration 45; owner, verbatim: *"if email said to provide API, but in later email that API was provided and brain didnt get it"* — measured: Safi Ullah delivered the DAL Open API **08-24 05:56**; Brain minted *"Share DAL Open API to Basit"* **08-26**, two days AFTER delivery, and chased him for a deadline on it) | **SYMPTOM TAG: `thread-answered-but-item-open` (the analyst's new tag, 1st — closed at the class).** An item is minted from ONE message and never reconciled against the thread that continued; worse, the continuation may be judged not-actionable on its own and `skipped` (the GCP/Imperial reply, 08-27 05:32), so the fulfilment is never even looked at | `open_items.source_feed_event_id` is a single event id; NOTHING re-examined a thread after the item existed. The analyst ranked this the top unfunded capability — Self Follow-Up / Thinking — and explicitly a JUDGEMENT ("auto-close is a judgement, not a lookup") | **Thread reconciliation runs FIRST in `emailItemDerivationJob`** — before the noise skip and before the actionability judgement. Cheap DB check (same-thread ACTIVE item via the source event's `threadId`; zero LLM cost when none); on a match the MODEL judges the relation — `fulfils` / `advances` / `unrelated` (the judgement is the model's, per the governance ruling). Code executes through guardrails: `fulfils` → the CANONICAL `transitionStatus(→CLOSED)` with the model's note as the resolution reason — ledgered, reversible, and **a REFUSED close (approval/lifecycle guard) falls back to ANNOTATION, never a forced write**; `advances` → annotation. Every outcome leaves a receipt on the item (`metadata.threadUpdates`: feedEventId, relation, note, closed, at) and the event exits `new` as `processed`. New items now also record `derivedFrom.threadId` so future matching needs no join. Reconciliation calls count against the same per-tenant batch cap. Guardrail: **no-claim-without-a-receipt** — the state change carries the message that caused it | this iteration | **NOT YET at build time** (Builder does not deploy) | **CODE-COMPLETE.** 6 tests on the DAL fixture (`def250ThreadAnsweredItemCloses.test.ts`): fulfils→canonical close+receipts+no new item+one LLM call · refused close→annotate only · advances→annotate no close · unrelated→falls through · no thread match→zero reconciliation cost · failed judgement→event stays `new`. Mutation RUN and killed: reconciliation branch disabled → 4 of 6 fail (the two no-op cases correctly survive); restore grep-verified. **Also repaired in this turn, with attribution:** `commImageSeam.test.ts` (b97225f, another session) shipped RED — its two failure-path tests mocked Gemini but not the OpenAI FALLBACK, constructing a real client that hung to timeout; the fallback is now mocked to fail fast (test-file-only change; 20s→0.6s, 5/5). Matrix AFTER both: tsc exit 0 · vitest 2966 passed 0 failed (of 2995) · diff clean. **CAPABILITY: Self Follow-Up · PREDICTED: a delivered answer closes or advances its item without the owner asking — the DAL shape becomes inexpressible, and the skipped-continuation blind spot is gone because reconciliation looks before the skip · MECHANISM: the thread is re-examined at the one consumer that sees every new message, judged by the model, executed through the lifecycle · FALSIFIER: any post-deploy item still chasing a thread whose fulfilment arrived in-window; or a reconciliation close with no `threadUpdates` receipt; or an item closed against a message the model judged `unrelated`** |
| **DEF-249** | 08-27 (iteration 43; Session 22, 08-26 10:06 — the newest live failure: *"delegate it to Madeeha Rahat"* → preview → confirmed TWICE → *"I need you to tell me the exact contact's name or a valid email"*) | **SYMPTOM TAG: `confirm-misfires` (7 in §3; S22 is the live 8th) — and the class is `protection-with-two-implementations` (10 deep): a rule enforced on one path, absent from the sibling that runs** | The single-action preview promised an action the dispatcher could not execute: `verifyActionTargets` — the ground-or-ask guard — ran only inside `dispatchPendingDirect`, AFTER his "yes" (`actionTargetGuard.ts:129` fired the refusal). | **DEF-247** | 08-26 (iteration 42; the Comm surface caught the class eating its own fix: Anthropic no-reply *"You have new requests from your team"* → `processed` + item at **08-26 01:05, AFTER the noise fix deployed**) | **SYMPTOM TAG: `noise-becomes-actionable` (the analyst's new tag, 1st) — and the class is `protection-with-two-implementations` (10 deep, this its 11th/12th instance).** Owner, CL-038: *"there are many noising or subsriber emails will brain consider those for actionable items?"* The fix shipped for that (7a36f03) wired `isLikelyAutomated` into `autonomousExecutor` — while `emailItemDerivationJob`, the job that actually mints gmail items, never called it (zero references, grep-confirmed by the analyst). A guard on one write path, minting on another | Sender-quality lived as scattered partial copies (triageSuggester, autonomousExecutor, a cleanup script) and was ABSENT from the one chokepoint every feed-derived create already passes through: `gateOpenItemCreate` — the same generator as DEF-245's LID guard gap | **The gate is now the enforcement point** (`openItemGate.ts`, step 1, reusing the feed-event sender it ALREADY loads for the muted-contact check): a feed-derived create from an `isLikelyAutomated` sender is BLOCKED with a structured reason — so every creator, present and future (gchat next), inherits the rule, and "wired in one place, absent at the write path" stops being expressible for this family. `emailItemDerivationJob` additionally calls the SAME shared predicate early so a newsletter never costs an LLM judgement (receipt `skipped`) — an optimization, never the enforcement; a creator that forgets it is still blocked at the gate. Fail-open on unreadable sender data (the pre-fix behaviour; blocking on a failed read would be `guard-destroys-the-answer`). | **DEF-246** | 08-26 (iteration 40; owner, verbatim, TWICE — S20/S23: *"Derive actionable or open items from each email arrive and add them"*) | **NO OPEN DECK TAG — the deck is empty; said plainly. This is THE ASK's top unfulfilled row: the capability itself.** gmail stood at **2,621 received / 0 EVER processed** while whatsapp/gcal/gtasks feeds run at 95–98% — a consumer existed for every channel except the one he asked about. DEF-243 (deployed) stopped Brain CLAIMING this capability; nothing until now BUILT it | No consumer derived items from gmail feed_events. | | | | | **↻ COMPLETED 08-13, iteration 17 (Codex iteration-16 verdict WEAK, directive #1).** Iteration 16 removed the wrong lookup but kept the SHAPE: routing resolved identity in `resolveByLidAlias`, the notice resolved it again in `resolveSenderDisplayName`, so the two could still disagree — and Codex found three ways they do. **One was a regression I introduced:** a first-time LID whose upstream mapping SUCCEEDED was reported as an unsaved contact, because my kind-check refused to read the real phone sitting in `fromIdentifier`. Also fixed: a failed alias write no longer splits the answer (the inference is returned either way, `bindingDurable: false` records it), and display policy is one rule — name, else the resolved number, else the fail-closed label, so a saved contact with a blank name is no longer called a stranger. My early-confirm guard deliberately bypassed that branch to fix the ordering — and silently bypassed its protections with it. No test caught it because every existing test exercised the branch that still had them. DEF-035 is what made this reachable at all: before it, "send" never dispatched, so nothing could be duplicated. **The fix for one defect created a worse one** | `dispatchConfirmedPending` extracted as the single chokepoint — artifact transitions + idempotency + terminal marking + throw handling in one place; BOTH confirm paths call it. `dispatchPendingDirect` now has exactly one external caller, CI-enforced by `def039SingleDispatchChokepoint.test.ts` (6 tests). | **DEF-181** | 08-16 (audit, **not owner-reported** — orchestrator read-only sweep; independently cross-checked against `git log -S` by a second session) | **The 7-day reset undo is silently unavailable.** Not owner-visible as a message. `POST /brain/reset` archives rows into `<table>_wipe_<userId>_<ms>` tables and `brainResetArchiveCleanup` is supposed to drop them after 7 days. **14 orphan tables, 140 rows, 245,760 bytes, 85 days old** survive against that retention, and both `brain_resets` runtime rows have `archive_suffix = NULL` — the reaper ran, reported success, dropped nothing for 4 of 8 families, and cleared the only pointer that could ever find them again | **A regression introduced at `bbfeec3`, which replaced working code.** `c8db3a7` composed `` `${prefix.replace(/_wipe_$/,'')}_${suffix}` `` and matched. `bbfeec3` changed it to `` `${prefix}${suffix}` `` (`brainResetArchiveCleanup.ts:48`) while adding the form-B creator sites. `archiveSuffix` (`brainResetRoutes.ts:129`) already begins `wipe_` and every prefix already ends `_wipe_`, so the four form-A families resolve to `<table>_wipe_wipe_…`, which never existed. `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS` converts the miss into success, `tablesDropped += 1` counts attempts rather than drops, and `archiveSuffix` is then nulled so it never retries. The comment at `:46-49` blames "a previous bogus replace" — `git log -S` returns only two commits and **no such version ever existed**; the only replace in the history was the correct one | **Structural, and explicitly NOT a revert.** Neither composition reaches both forms (form A needs the replace, form B needs the concat), so restoring `c8db3a7` would strand the families still being minted — strictly worse than today on new resets. Mint and reap the name from ONE function; normalise the seven creator sites (`brainResetRoutes.ts` L165/177/187/196/218/228/244) to a single form; carry legacy prefixes as an explicit migration list rather than as a shape to pattern-match. The 14 existing orphans need a manual drop — their pointers are already gone | **OPEN — not fixed; nothing was modified.** §3 verdict: **CIRCLING** — the mechanism is `protection-with-two-implementations` (§3 count 10), so a patch on the reported instance is the wrong move. Note the origin is a *regression* while the mechanism is a *known class*; both are true. Evidence: SOURCE_INSPECTION + GIT_OBJECT + read-only production `SELECT`. | **DEF-034** | 08-05 11:56, **RECURRED 08-05 14:41** | Brain said "Hold on — I hadn't actually done that yet; nothing was executed on my end. Say the word and I'll do it now" **one second after successfully delegating three items** | **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 14:41, and my own DEF-041 fix is what exposed it.** Chain: send #1 dispatched and marked the pending terminal → send #2 found no active pending → fell through to reasoning → reasoning re-claimed the completion → **the empty-promise rule fired (correctly, now that DEF-041 un-exempted it)** → blocked the answer with `[no action dispatched…]` → `answerSanitizer.ts:39` renders that marker as **"nothing was executed on my end"**. The marker is TURN-scoped ("no action dispatched *in this turn*"); its human rendering is WORLD-scoped ("nothing was executed"). Both statements are about different things and only the first is true. Exactly the DEF-037 provenance failure: the system cannot distinguish *I didn't do this in this turn* from *this was not done*. **Note honestly: before DEF-041 this rule never fired on the reasoning path, so my fix increased the frequency of this false denial** | (a) Reword the marker to be turn-scoped — "I didn't run anything just now", never "nothing was executed"; (b) before emitting it, read the dispatch ledger for a recent completion of this pending — if the work IS done, the honest answer is "that's already done", not a denial; (c) **never offer "say the word and I'll do it now" for work already completed** — that is an invitation to duplicate a real external action | `PENDING` | **NOT YET** — built and verified in-tree this session; deploy follows in the same session | **PARTIALLY RESOLVED 08-19 20:20 PKT — (a) and (c) built and verified; (b) BUILT iteration 31, 2026-08-24 (see below).** The root cause was found 08-05 14:41 and **nothing was ever built for it**, which is why it kept firing: **21 occurrences in the 14 days to 08-19**, the largest live signature on the owner's own deck, and the reason he asked "if x21 times mean this occure 21 times and still i am seeing this means cron is unable to fix it". **(a) SCOPE — done.** `answerSanitizer.ts:46` no longer renders a TURN-scoped marker in WORLD-scoped words: *"I didn't run anything just now, so I can't tell you it's done."* **(c) DUPLICATION — done.** The unconditional *"Say the word and I'll do it now"* is gone; it now promises to CHECK before acting, the only honest offer from a position of admitted ignorance, and one that cannot invite a second irreversible external action. **(b) LEDGER READ — BUILT, iteration 31 (2026-08-24), 19 days after diagnosis — `SYMPTOM TAG: `fabricated-status`` (6 deep, the class this closes at its generator).** `CompletionGateInput.recentDispatches` carries completed dispatches read from the EXISTING `getRecentDispatches` ledger (no second implementation), fetched at both call sites only when the answer carries a claim shape and bounded to 15 minutes (the diagnosed failure was a re-ask ONE SECOND after dispatch; a stale receipt must not license an unrelated claim). Every intercept now checks the receipt first: with one, the replacement is `ledgerReadbackMarker` — constructor + curated renderer in `actionReceipt.ts`, one module (DEF-238's contract) — so what reaches him is the recorded fact ("that already ran, at T"), model-phrased; with none, the three pre-(b) behaviours are byte-identical (asserted). A FAILED dispatch licenses nothing (DEF-237's rule survives, asserted). Guardrail: **no-claim-without-a-receipt, in both directions** — no "done" without a receipt, no "nothing ran" against one. 8 tests (`def034bReadBackBeforeClaim.test.ts`), 2 mutants RUN and killed (receipt-matching disabled → 2 fail; renderer hook dropped → 1 fail). **CAPABILITY: Accurate Communication · PREDICTED: the 2026-08-05 shape (a denial one second after real work) is no longer expressible when the ledger holds the receipt · MECHANISM: the gate reads before it denies · FALSIFIER: any post-deploy transcript where a completion claim within 15 min of a succeeded dispatch is answered with "nothing was executed"/"no action ran"** — the original spec was: before emitting the marker, read the dispatch ledger and answer *"that's already done"* when it is. Neither call site (`brainComposer.ts:2760`, `:3500`) has recent-dispatch context in scope, so this needs it plumbed into `CompletionGateInput` — a structural change on the messaging path, not a wording change, and not smuggled into this one. **VERIFIED BY A/B, not by assertion:** `def034MarkerScopeMustMatchClaimScope.test.ts` guards the CLASS (no turn-scoped marker may render a world-scoped claim, and none may offer a blind redo) — **6 of 9 fail against the pre-fix wording, 9 pass after**. Full matrix after the last edit: `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0 · `npx vitest run` **2817 passed, 0 failed** (7 expected-fail, 21 skipped, 1 todo, 2846 total) · `git diff --check` clean. **LIVE ACCEPTANCE STILL OWED** — this is on a messaging path, so DEPLOYED is not VERIFIED. Falsifier: any occurrence of "nothing was executed" in a transcript after this deploy, which the deck now counts as `since` against the deploy time. |

## C. Builder-process defects (mine, not Brain's)

Recorded because they cost the owner real time and must not repeat.

| ID | Date | What I did wrong | Consequence | Correction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BLD-001 | 08-05 11:40 | Used `git add server/docs/` (directory add) instead of naming files | Committed and pushed `server/docs/nexeo_self_learning&development.md` — a file AGENTS.md explicitly forbids ever committing — and it landed on production via the pull | `git rm --cached` (kept on disk), added to `.gitignore`. **Rule: always name files in `git add`, never a directory.** History still contains it; purge available on request |
| BLD-002 | 07-28→08-04 | Carried the open-defect queue in conversation instead of a document | Made months of real progress look like circling to the owner | The three-doc protocol |
| BLD-003 | 08-04 | Reported a suite figure measured BEFORE the final edit | Claimed green while the tree was red | Re-run after the last change; quote commit-stamped numbers |
| **BLD-005** | 08-05 13:12 | Wrote a branch that deliberately bypasses another branch, and did not check what protections that branch was carrying. The DEF-035 guard skipped the confirm reducer to fix ordering — and skipped its idempotency wrapper and artifact ledger with it | Shipped a duplicate-send path to production for ~30 minutes and told the owner to run a "send" test on it. An external audit caught it, not me and not 1,272 tests | **When bypassing a code path, enumerate what it does BEYOND the thing you are bypassing.** A guard that lives inside one caller is not a guard. Structural answer: extract the chokepoint and CI-enforce a single caller, rather than duplicating the protections into the new branch |
| BLD-004 | 08-04 | Left DEF-024's fix pushed but undeployed while diagnosing other things | The same defect hit the owner three separate times | Deploy blockers before starting new diagnosis |
| **BLD-006** | 08-10 | Shipped DEF-115 and DEF-116 with code, tests and full commit messages — and **no row in this log.** Rule 2 says every change moves a row; two changes moved none, and the commit message became the only record | The exact failure BLD-002 exists to prevent, one layer down. A defect whose only record is a commit message is invisible to the recurrence table, so the next instance of its class reads as first-occurrence and gets patched again instead of structurally | Rows added retroactively 08-11 (above), marked with their real verification state. **The log row goes in the same commit as the code, not after it** — a commit message is evidence, not a ledger |
| **BLD-008** | 08-11 14:53 → caught 15:00 | Rewrote `changeStatus` to pass `actor: 'system'` **while it still accepted an `actor` argument** — every caller's identity silently discarded. **It shipped**: the collapse is in `41dfdff` and went to production in D-22 before I read my own diff | **Two failures, one line, live.** The ledger DEF-127 had just restored now records `system` for every human and agent status change through that path — so the audit trail exists and lies about who acted. And `is_owner_or_agent` treats `'system'` as always authorised, so within a tenant a non-owner could move another user's item through `POST /open-items/:id/status`, including the new CANCELLED→TRIAGED restore where that guard is the only protection. Tracked as **DEF-133** because it is a live defect, not just a process slip | Pass-through restored, threaded onto `delegateItem` and its three call sites. **Mutation-verified: re-introducing the collapse turns 3 assertions red, including a named non-owner-restore test.** **Rule: a parameter that is accepted and then ignored is worse than one never offered** — it reads as honoured at every call site, which is exactly why the diff passed review. **And the deeper one: I ran the verification matrix and shipped inside the window in which I had not yet read my own diff.** Green tests do not review intent |
| **BLD-009** | 08-11 14:53 → caught 15:05 | Introduced `INACTIVE_STATUS_VALUES` documented as *"the ONE definition of no longer on the user's plate"*, plus an `INACTIVE_STATUS_SQL` beside it — and left **all seven raw-SQL queries hand-writing their own lists**, with the SQL constant referenced by nothing at all. Also shipped in `41dfdff` | **The 7th `protection-with-two-implementations` — authored by me, in the very change meant to consolidate.** Per chart §3 this tag is *circling*, not progressing: a constant asserting it is canonical while the call sites prove it is not is precisely the comforting fiction that shape is made of, and a dead export is what makes review read the job as done | All seven sites now compose from `INACTIVE_STATUS_SQL` (which also closed a latent hole: they had never excluded the legacy lowercase spellings, so legacy-cased disposed rows were still being chased by the radar and the criticality engine). Containment is **CI-enforced**: a source guard fails on any `status NOT IN ('CLOSED'…)` literal in those files and asserts each references the shared constant — **comments stripped before matching**, because four earlier source guards here passed by matching the prose that described them. Mutation-verified by reverting one site |
| **BLD-010** | 08-11 15:10 | Widened the ALREADY-APPLIED migration `20260811_normalise_legacy_done_status` in place, rather than adding a new one. It had run on production 20 minutes earlier (D-22, 6 rows) | A migration file that no longer describes what the database executed. The tree would have claimed a 16-spelling repair where production ran a one-spelling repair — precisely the tree-versus-reality drift these hand-written files exist to prevent, and undetectable afterwards | Reverted the applied file untouched; the widened repair is a NEW folder (`20260811b_…`, DEF-132). **Two assertions now pin the boundary**: the applied file must not mention DEF-132 or its temp table. **Rule: an applied migration is append-only — widen by adding a file, never by editing one** |
| **BLD-007** | 08-10 | Reported wiki memory as fully indexed — *"all 12,473/12,473 pages embedded, 0 unreachable"* — measured with `count(embedding)` | The number was true and the claim was false: 63% of those vectors were `stub-768` placeholders, so retrieval was returning confident nonsense over most of Brain's memory while I reported it healthy. Cost 21 hours during which the system's own finding said otherwise every 50 minutes (DEF-118) | **A presence check is not a validity check.** Count what the column MEANS, not whether it is filled — for embeddings that is `embedding_model = <the model we search with>`. Same lesson as reporting `n_live_tup` estimates as row counts on 08-11 |
